The rest of the original init tail (`timelimit 0`, `g_synchronousClients 0`, `defrag_svfpsInitialized` on dedicated) is still not forced. - **User request: force `timelimit 0` and `g_synchronousClients 0` (BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, under `df_enforceSvFps`)**: completes the port of the `0x21fc` init tail (Q4 note: `g_synchronousClients` default stays 0 in oDFs, now also forced). In `G_ResolveDfMode` (g_spawn.c), with `df_enforceSvFps 1` (default): non-dedicated -> `sv_fps` 125 when outside 60..140 (cheats off) and **`timelimit 0` on every map load** (original `0x23d5`, not cheat-gated); dedicated / variant -> once per server run (guard cvar `defrag_svfpsInitialized`): `sv_fps 125`, **`g_synchronousClients 0`** (original `0x238a-0x23ac`). Revert / opt out: `set df_enforceSvFps 0`. Build clean, untested.

Q4 resolved: `g_synchronousClients` and `pmove_fixed` now default to `1` like DeFRaG (`g_main.c`; BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: fixed 8 ms pmove steps and synchronous clients on a server whose config does not set them; the dedicated one-time init under `df_enforceSvFps` still sets `g_synchronousClients 0`). Revert: defaults back to `"0"`. Q2 / Q3 / Q5 / Q6 / Q7 explained to the user, decisions pending. Build clean, untested.
- **Open questions, second round (user, 2026-10-24)**: Q3 accepted (Armor Used stays 0), Q5 accepted, Q7 accepted (the port aims at the triggering player). **Q6 resolved: forced gametype PORTED (BEHAVIOUR CHANGE)**: new cvar `df_forceGametype` (default 1, ARCHIVE; g_main.c). In `G_ResolveDfMode` (g_spawn.c), right after the mode is known and before the ndm presets (as in `0x21fc`), `g_gametype` is set to `4` (CTF) for mode 3 (fastcaps) and `0` (free for all) for every other mode, and the cached `g_gametype.integer` is refreshed with `trap_Cvar_Update` (the original stores into its cached value directly). Effect: a fastcaps map always runs as CTF (teams, flags), every other map as FFA, whatever the server had configured; the cgame sees the new value through serverinfo on connect. Revert / opt out: `set df_forceGametype 0`. Q2 (com_maxfps clamp) explained, decision pending. Build clean, untested.
- **Q2 resolved by the user: client `com_maxfps` clamp PORTED (BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, cgame)**: new client cvar `df_enforceMaxFps` (default 1, ARCHIVE; cg_main.c). `CG_DF_EnforceMaxFps` (cg_defrag.c, called every frame at the end of `CG_DF_GhostFrame`) = cgame `0x1c0b-0x1c50` of the per-frame driver `0x1bd3`: while `sv_cheats` is off (`0x11d40c` = our `CG_DF_Cheats()`; this is the cheats flag, not the `0x11d2ac` version gate) `com_maxfps` is kept inside 60..140 (0 = unlimited is raised to 140) and the red notice `com_maxfps highest value restricted to 140.` / `lowest value restricted to 60.` is printed. Differences from the original: only on DeFRaG servers (`o3j_version` / `defrag_vers` published, i.e. oDFs servers too), never in demo playback, and the notice is printed locally (the original sends `Notify "<text>"` to the server, whose `notify` handler echoes it back as a `print`). Revert / opt out: `set df_enforceMaxFps 0`. Build clean, untested. All open questions Q1-Q7 are now closed.
- **Playtest bug (user): target_fragsFilter "N more frags needed" always shown -- FIXED (polarity)**: the use handler (`0x2963`, `0x299c`: `if ( ( spawnflags & 4 ) == 0 ) goto message; else return`) shows the `cpsm "%i more frag(s) needed"` text when spawnflag 4 is NOT set and stays silent when it is; iter 33 (and the first port) had it the other way round (text only with flag 4), so on maps that set flag 4 to hide the message it appeared at map load / after every frag. Now `if ( spawnflags & 4 ) return;`, QUAKED comment says HIDE_NEEDED. Note: a map without flag 4 will show the text (as in the original). Revert: restore `!( ... & 4 )`. Build clean, untested.
- **Playtest bug (user): "defrag setup" CHS and Q3 pages -> "menu_additem: excessive items" -- FIXED**: `MAX_MENUITEMS` of the q3_ui menu framework was the stock 64, but one DeFRaG setup menu holds the 8 nav buttons + the page spinner + every widget of the page: CHS 60 + 9 = 69, Q3 extras 73 + 9 = 82 (the other pages are 6..42 + 9, fine). `Menu_AddItem` refused the items past 64 and printed the error. `MAX_MENUITEMS` raised to 256 in `code/q3_ui/ui_local.h` (the widget array of the setup menu, `UI_DF_MAXITEMS` 96, already fits 73). The original ui.qvm has its own limit (not decoded). Revert: set it back to 64 (and the two pages stop working). Build clean, untested -- repack the pk3.
- **Playtest bug (user): holding `kill` + `attack` respawns "insanely frequently" -- FIXED (respawn button is edge-triggered)**: in the dead-player block of the original (inlined in Pmove outer `0x1a866`, `0x1ac35-0x1ac4a`) the attack / use-holdable respawn needs `( ucmd->buttons & 5 ) != 0` AND `( client->oldbuttons & 5 ) == 0`, i.e. the button must be newly pressed; `oldbuttons` already holds the previous frame (the swap/latch runs earlier). The port respawned whenever the button was merely held, so with a key-repeating `kill` and a held `attack` the player was killed, respawned after `df_cl_respawnMsec` (default 250 ms), killed again... Now `ClientThink_real` (g_active.c) requires the press edge; the `g_forcerespawn` path is unchanged. Side effect (same as the original): dying while holding attack needs a release and a new press to respawn. Revert: drop the `oldbuttons` condition. Build clean, untested. (Audit gap: the earlier ClientRespawn doc compared this block with the port and recorded the button test as identical; the `oldbuttons` test at `0x1ac40` was missed.)
- **Re-audit cgame/ui iter 1 (target 1: checkpoint / finish / record events: cgame `0x5248` run state machine, `0x1c8b` event producer, `0x50b6` popup, `0x5153` list, `0x4f9d` summary, `0x1024` / `0x10c8` formatters; port cg_dfrecords.c + cg_defrag.c) -- AUDITED: FIXED (BEHAVIOUR CHANGE)**: identical: `0x1024` (S:mmm / M:SS:mmm, sign), `0x10c8` as used (colour `2` / `1` / `7`, always-shown sign, same widths), the popup text rules (delta only for mode 2 with a started run and a reference, total for mode 3, split / first time otherwise, `B[-1]` quirk), the checkpoint step (`B[N] = t`, popup before `N++`, `N + 1 < 32`), the finish record logic (spectator skip, `latest` file, new / tie flags on event bits 512 / 1024, tie overwrite cvar, dirty flag), summary `0x4f9d`, the HUD list window (last 8). Event word decoded (`0x1c8b`): 0x1 first snapshot / map load, 0x10 respawn (spawn-count change, also set with map load / server count), 0x800 viewed client changed (`ps.clientNum`), 0x40 start, 0x80 checkpoint, 0x100 stop. **Fixed 1:** `0x5273-0x52a1` runs its reset block on start (0x40) **or respawn (0x10) or viewed-client change (0x800)**: pending record -> R2, reload R1, **clear the current run `B`**, and `runStarted = (event 0x40)`; the port did this only on a start, so after a respawn the old checkpoint list stayed (and the vanilla-HUD list `df_hud 0` kept drawing it). New `CG_DF_RecReset( started )`; `CG_DF_ScriptEvents` calls `RecReset( qfalse )` on every respawn event (also in demos). **Fixed 2:** the whole finish block (record files and the final popup, `0x52f3`) only runs when a start was seen since the last reset (`runStarted`); the port ran it for any finish (e.g. following a player mid-run). `RecInit` now clears the flag. **Fixed 3:** the `df_recfiles_session` change check runs every snapshot (`0x524d`), not only at a start. Not changed (deliberate): checkpoint popups stay visible with sv_cheats (user choice; original skips them). Revert: restore the old `CG_DF_RecStart` body / remove the `CG_DF_RecReset( qfalse )` call. Build clean, untested.
- **Re-audit cgame/ui iter 2 (target 2: records subsystem: template expander `0x586d`, variable expander `0x5543` / `0x5504`, value copier `0x5431`, record read `0x4e9c` / checksum `0x4e75` / write `0x4f46`, loaders `0x506c` / `0x5092`) -- AUDITED: FIXED (BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: record file names)**: identical: the fixed template `$map_$-route_$gt_$phys_$-mode` + extension `rec`, directory handling (the original tests `dir[-1]` instead of the last character of the directory, a bug that only shows for a directory that already ends in `/`; not reproduced), reserved-space accounting, colour-code skipping and the file-name character filter of the template text (`<= 32`, `>= 126`, `: * ? " < > | \ /`), `$gt` table (`ac df tm fc res mdf mtm mfc`), `$phys` (`vq3` / `cpm`), `$mode` (one digit, empty when negative), `$route`, `$plc` (df_country), the `$-` / `$+` empty-variable separator rules (`0x5504`), record file format (v2 9 ints with the count moved to +128, v3 33 ints, byte checksum over the payload, size limit 512, version byte 3 on write), `R2` directory (`system/records[/demos]`), `R1` path (`system/records/<df_recfiles_session>`), `latest`. **Fixed 1:** values copied by `0x5431` (`$map`, `$phys`, `$gt`, `$plc`, `$pl`, `$route`, `$mode`) are filtered differently from the template text: **colour codes are dropped** (not counted against the length limit) and **`. ( ) [ ]` also become `-`**; the port copied `$pl` / `$plc` / `$map` raw (so a player called `^1Foo` produced `^1Foo` in the file name instead of `Foo`). `CG_DF_NamePut` now does what `0x5431` does. **Fixed 2:** the write-failure message is `SaveRecord(): failed to open %s for writing.` (port: `SetRecord(): ... "%s" ...`). Not ported (the record templates do not use them): the time / date variables `$s $ms $m $datetime $date` of `0x5543` (autorecord has its own expander). Revert: restore the old `CG_DF_NamePut` / message. Build clean, untested.

port cg_dfhud.c + cg_defrag.c) -- AUDITED: FIXED (BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: HUD speed / Max Speed)**: identical: `0x259a` (the slide-in / slide-out states are dead code, a constant 0.0 at data `0x12d0` disables them: the box is drawn at its x / y when visible and its state set to 0 when hidden; warning colour yellow for `df_hud_color 1` else red when `value < warn`; inner box with `df_hud_transparency`, border with `df_hud_frameTransparency`; icon at +10 / 16x16, text start `x + 26`; text right-aligned by `(width - len) * 16`; value form `%3i` / `%2i` at `x + 36`), colour string parser `0x24ac` (digit runs of at most 3, clamped to 1.0, the character after each run skipped) and lookup `0x2559` (custom colour unless its red component is negative, palettes 0..7 / 8..12 checked against the float data, white default), the whole init `0x2fdd` (every widget position, size, warn threshold, width, icon and box shader: best 78x32, last / speed 116x32, power-ups 78x32, flag / holdable 36x32, health / armor / ammo 94x32, frags 126x32, checkpoint 256 wide; shader names), team colours (red 0.9 .05 .05, blue 0 0 1, only with `df_hud_forceteamcolors`), best-time box (visible = `df_drawBesttime`, 164x32 box and width 9 above 59999 ms), last-time box, power-up list (ids 1..6, 3 slots, `(t - time) / 1000 + 1` capped at 99), flag box, holdable box, health / armor / ammo (warn table for weapons 2..9 read from the jump table at `0x1334`) / frags (`%2i/%2i`, hidden by `defrag_flags & 1`), checkpoint box (7-line window, 164 vs 256 wide at |summary| > 59999). **Fixed 1:** the HUD speed is `0x3a4a`'s value: the **PREDICTED** player state's horizontal speed (`cg.predictedPlayerState.velocity`, z zeroed), **truncated to an integer**, computed once per frame in `CG_DrawActiveFrame` before the draw (`0x29e32`); the port used the snapshot velocity as a float. New `CG_DF_SpeedFrame` (cg_defrag.c, called from cg_view.c) feeds the speed widget (`CG_DF_SpeedText( (float)dfSpeedInt )`) and the `Max Speed` statistic (raised every frame while a run is active, was once per snapshot). **Fixed 2:** an expired power-up is removed without re-checking the entry that moved up into its place (`0x2b86`), the port re-checked it with `i--` (a second power-up expiring in the same frame is now removed one frame later, as in DeFRaG). port cg_dfdemocam.c, cg_dfreplay.c `CG_DF_CamOffsets`) -- AUDITED: FIXED (8 divergences)**: identical: cvar clamps, transition maths (time / distance based, clamp, A->B lerp, angle lerp), 4..7 saved-position target (`saveposname`, +26 eye) and third-person fallback, modes 9 / 10 / 11 chase offsets, free camera (init, usercmd angle bookkeeping, 5 * |speed| / 100 * dt movement), hide-body table (modes 2 and 8), third-person-forcing table, wall trace box, replay-camera entry `0x15550`. **Fixed 1:** aim table `0x14f18` was reversed for 8 vs 9..11: modes 9 / 10 / 11 aim fully at the target (`vectoangles(target - camera)`), mode 8 keeps its angles (the port kept the angles for 9..11, so the chase camera looked at the point 512 units ahead of the player instead of at the player). **Fixed 2:** `0x153ec` aims in place: the aimed angles replace `angCur` (so the next transition starts from them), then the position / radius offsets are applied to the output; if THEY moved the camera, the camera aims again from the displaced position and the angle offsets are added a second time (`0x15246` returns position-or-radius changed, angle offsets do not count). **Fixed 3:** the `targetposname` cvar (name of a savepos variable) replaces the look-at point (`track.pos2`) of every mode (`0x7d59(targetposname)` inside `0x1549d`) -- was listed as not ported. **Fixed 4:** the tracked-client record, hide list, third-person gate and wall traces compare / skip `cg.predictedPlayerState.clientNum` (the port used `cg.clientNum`); the hide list now keys on the client tracked in the last camera frame. **Fixed 5:** mode 9 always uses transition speed 200 in the demo wrapper too (`0x15027`), as the replay camera does. **Fixed 6:** `0x14741`: a dead player's chase camera uses `STAT_DEAD_YAW`; the wall trace is skipped with `com_cameraMode`. **Fixed 7:** mode changes no longer reset the camera to the current view: the original keeps the last pose (modes 1 / 2 / 3 / 8 and the 4..7 fallback write all four pose slots every frame), so switching from mode 8 to 4 starts at the old pose and the FIRST activation after a cgame start (zeroed camera object) transitions from the world origin (0,0,0); dt is measured every frame by the wrapper even with mode 0 or outside demos (replay camera: only while it runs). **Fixed 8:** `dcLastClient` starts at 0 like the zeroed original track record. BEHAVIOUR CHANGES: 7 (first activation visibly flies in from the origin for modes 4..7 with a saved position and 9..11) and 1. - **Clock sanity flags survive map_restart (user request, qagame `0x3ccd` / `0x406d`)**: in the original only `0x3ccd` writes the sticky flags (`0x1320f4` / `0x1320f8`) and the accumulators / real-time sample (`0x1320dc` / `0x1320e0` / `0x1320e4`); the game init `0x406d` only stores `level.time` into `0x1320fc` (the start of the 2.5 s grace). `map_restart` reloads the game without unloading the module, so the flags and accumulators persist. `G_InitRaceMode` (g_defrag.c) now only sets `g_clockInitTime = level.time` instead of clearing all six variables, and the lazy init in `G_ClockSanityFrame` is gone. A new map still starts clean (the module is reloaded). **Behaviour change:** a bad frame / timescale flag set before a `map_restart` stays set. Revert: restore the single clearing line in `G_InitRaceMode` (`g_clockBadFrame = g_clockBadRatio = g_clockInitTime = g_clockGameAcc = g_clockRealAcc = g_clockPrevReal = 0;`) and the lazy `if ( !g_clockInitTime )` init. Build clean (qagame.qvm), untested; repack the pk3.
- **Snapshot events at read time (user request; cgame `0x1e4c` called from `CG_ReadNextSnapshot`)**: the original calls `0x1e4c( newSnap, cg.snap )` right after a snapshot is read successfully (before the lagometer entry): if the new snapshot has `SNAPFLAG_NOT_ACTIVE` (flag 2) nothing happens, otherwise the event word `0x1c8b`, the validity checks `0x6237` and the per-frame driver `0x1bd3` run for the pair (current, new). The port ran `CG_DF_ScriptEvents` + `CG_DF_SnapshotTransition` in `CG_TransitionSnapshot` (up to one snapshot interval later). **Now:** new `CG_DF_ReadSnapshot( old, cur )` (cg_defrag.c) is called from `CG_ReadNextSnapshot` (cg_snapshot.c) when `cg.snap` exists and the new snapshot is active; it runs the same two functions with the pair (cg.snap, new). The first snapshot still goes through `CG_SetInitialSnapshot` (event 0x11). Two things had to follow: (1) `CG_DF_RaceTime` decodes the race time with the serverTime of the snapshot the player state belongs to; it used `cg.snap->serverTime`, which at read time is the OLD snapshot, so the new static `dfDecodeTime` carries the serverTime of the snapshot being read (0 elsewhere = old behaviour for the HUD); the haste test in the validator uses it too. (2) The end-of-run stats block after a finish used to be printed after the finish's `stats` server command had cleared `runActive` (commands run at the transition, before the old event code); with events at read time the order flips and the block would print twice on servers that send `stats`. The print is therefore deferred: the finish event sets `dfFinishPrint`, and `CG_DF_FlushFinishPrint()` (called in `CG_TransitionSnapshot` where the events used to run) prints once if `runActive` is still set. All other effects of start / checkpoint / finish / death / respawn (records, popups, ghosts, autorecord, exec scripts, `cg.weaponSelect`, validity reports) now happen up to one snapshot earlier, as in the original; the death stats print stays at read time. The race time shown and recorded is unchanged (it comes from the snapshot's own player state). **Behaviour change / risk:** touches the code path of the confirmed timer / checkpoint / autorecord behaviour; test those first. Revert: in `CG_ReadNextSnapshot` remove the `CG_DF_ReadSnapshot` call and in `CG_TransitionSnapshot` replace `CG_DF_FlushFinishPrint();` by `CG_DF_ScriptEvents( oldFrame, cg.snap ); CG_DF_SnapshotTransition( ops, ps );` (the deferred print is then harmless). Build clean (all three), untested; repack the pk3.
- **Bsp crc check ported (user request; qagame `0x2d02` + forced `defrag_bspcrc` of `0x1b57`, cgame `0x61c6` / `0x63f5` / `0x6222` / `0x5dc1`)**: corrects the old claim (UNPORTED.md, cheat_report.md) that the check only matters for gametype >= 5: `0x61c6` returns when `0x11d2ac != 0` (multiplayer, gametype >= 5), so it runs on the normal race gametypes, including oDFs. **Server:** `G_PublishMapChecksum` (g_spawn.c, called at the end of `G_ResolveDfMode` like `0x2d02` from the tail of `0x21fc`, outside the `df_enforceSvFps` block) sets the new SYSTEMINFO cvar `defrag_bspcrc` to `va( "%i", sv_mapChecksum )` and writes the `sv_mapChecksum` string (no terminator) to `system/checksums/<lower-case mapname>.crc`. **Client:** `CG_DF_LoadBspRecord` (called first in `CG_DF_InitCvars` = the `0x3985` sequence) reads that file unless the server is multiplayer: missing, unreadable or longer than 32 bytes -> violation 6 "Bsp crc: missing local record" (no record), else `atoi` of the content is the local record. `CG_DF_CheckBspCrc` runs at the end of the `ver >= 18100` block of the systeminfo validator (after g_knockback): a record exists and `atoi( defrag_bspcrc )` differs -> violation 5 "Bsp crc: checksum failed". Violations are logged for `svinfo_report` / `svinfo_summary` (first occurrence, not printed) like the other codes; `df_validityChecks 0` switches the load off too. A client that never hosted the map locally has no record and gets code 6 (as in the original). **Behaviour change:** new reports and a new file under `system/checksums/` on the server side. Revert: remove the `G_PublishMapChecksum` call and the `defrag_bspcrc` table entry; remove `CG_DF_LoadBspRecord()` / `CG_DF_CheckBspCrc(...)` calls. Build clean (qagame, cgame, ui), untested; repack the pk3.
- **Bsp crc: auth_request second argument (cgame `0x3d52` @ `0x3d84`)**: the dispatcher calls `4337( atoi( arg1 ) )` (auth reply) and then `6222( atoi( arg2 ) )` (the same bsp comparator as the systeminfo validator). `auth_request <token> <sv_mapChecksum>` is sent by our qagame when `df_auth 1`. Ported: `CG_DF_AuthBspCrc( atoi( argv 2 ) )` after `CG_DF_AuthRequest` (cg_servercmds.c, cg_defrag.c). The second `6222` call site (`0x3eae`) belongs to the legacy `NewTime` text command, which stays unported. Build clean, untested.
- **Client prediction: promode and overbounce kill came from fields only the server set (bg_pmove.c) -- FIXED**: found by the demo physics tests (testing/, `dfgolden steps`). The port's physics read `pm->promode` and `pm->obs_disabled`; only `ClientThink_real` set them, `cg_predict.c` never did, so client prediction ran VQ3 physics on CPM servers and ignored `df_ob_KillOBs`. The original reads the networked playerState instead: qagame `PM_Friction` (`0x66e0`) tests `ps->pm_flags & 0x8000` (`0x6760`), the original cgame's Pmove copy does the same (`PM_CheckJump` `0x2c9e4`, `0x2ca67`), and `func_0x2575` writes that bit from `df_promode` every frame; the overbounce kill uses the `STAT_DEFRAG_FLAGS & 0x200` bit (disasm/BG_PMOVE/PM_GroundTrace.md), which our landing clip already used. Fix: `Pmove()` sets `pmove->promode = ( ps->pm_flags & PMF_PROMODE ) != 0` before the move; the OBS kill tests `stats[STAT_DEFRAG_FLAGS] & DFLAG_OBS_DISABLED` (`pm->obs_disabled` stays as an extra source, server only). Server behaviour is unchanged (it writes both from the same cvars right before Pmove). Evidence: replaying 75,290 recorded steps of 20 CPM demos with promode handed to Pmove as 0 (the client's situation) matched 45.81% before the fix and 98.93% after (same as with the real setting). **Behaviour change:** cgame prediction on CPM servers now matches the server (fewer prediction errors). Revert: delete the `pmove->promode = ...` line at the top of `Pmove()` and restore `pm->obs_disabled ||` as the only OBS condition. Build clean (qagame, cgame, ui); repack the pk3.
- **CheckExitRules: a single player could end the level by reaching the fraglimit (g_main.c) -- FIXED**: found by replaying the demo doom2-01 through the real game (testing/, `dfgolden gamesteps`): after the 6th keycard the port printed "hit the fraglimit" and began the intermission, while the recording carries on. The original `CheckExitRules` (qagame `0xc24`) returns at `0xc73` unless `level.numPlayingClients >= 2` (as vanilla Q3 does), before the frag / capture limit checks; the port had dropped that test, so a solo racer on a `target_fragsFilter` map (which publishes the keycard count as `fraglimit`) ended the level. Added `if ( level.numPlayingClients < 2 ) return;` in front of the fraglimit block. **Behaviour change:** solo runs no longer end at the fraglimit; with two or more players nothing changes. Effect on the replay: doom2-01 81.6% -> 98.3% exact steps, fpscup03-6 75.4% -> 98.9%. Revert: delete the three lines. Build clean (qagame, cgame, ui); repack the pk3.
- **SnapVectorTowards used floor/ceil (ioq3) instead of the original's truncation (g_weapon.c) -- FIXED**: found by replaying the plasma-climb demo lucy_cup06 through the real game (testing/, `dfgolden gamesteps`): the spent plasma bolt entity (explosion position) was 1 unit off in y and z against the recording, which skewed the splash knockback direction (velocity off by up to ~5 ups per shot, damageYaw off by 2). The original `SnapVectorTowards` (qagame `0x289a6`) is vanilla 1.32: `if ( to[i] > v[i] ) v[i] = (int)v[i] + 1; else v[i] = (int)v[i];` (truncate toward zero); the port had the ioq3 version `floor`/`ceil`, which differs for negative coordinates and for integral values. Replaced with the original expressions. **Behaviour change:** impact points of missiles, rail / bullet hit points and hook points are rounded like the original; for positive coordinates the result is the same except exactly integral values (now +1 away from the start point like the original). Effect on the replay: lucy_cup06 98.24% -> 99.64% exact steps, plasma-gun steps 4/61 -> 51/61 exact, server knockback 0/52 -> 45/52. Revert: restore `floor(v[i])` / `ceil(v[i])` in `SnapVectorTowards`. Build clean (qagame, cgame, ui); repack the pk3.
- **DEFRAG flag bits of stats[12] (df_ob_KillOBs / df_hook_Type / df_lg_Knockback): packed per spawn and per weapon pickup, not per frame (g_active.c, g_client.c, g_items.c, g_weapon.c) -- FIXED**: found by replaying sl1k-inh3-ok through the real game (testing/, `dfgolden gamesteps`): the recording's stats[12] gets bit 0x1000 (LG knockback) at the moment the player picks up the map's lightning gun (the item carries `"speed" "1"`), and the port never did. The original calls `func_0x421b` (SetOBS 0x426b / SetGrappleMode 0x42b5 / SetLGKnockback 0x4290 from the three cvar caches) only from `ClientSpawn` (`0x1d576`, right after `func_0x274b`), and `Pickup_Weapon` (`0x20d2d`) calls `SetLGKnockback( ps, (int)item->speed )` for the lightning gun and `SetGrappleMode( ps, (int)item->speed )` for the grappling hook (the item's `speed` key: a map enables them per weapon). The lightning gun fire code (`0x291f4`) tests `ps.stats[12] & 0x1000` of the shooter (not the cvar). The port instead cleared and re-set the bits from the cvars every frame in `ClientThink_real` and tested the cvar in `Weapon_LightningFire`. Now: `G_SetDefragFlags` (OBS flag, grapple mode, LG knockback from the cvars) runs in `ClientSpawn` after the loadout; `Pickup_Weapon` sets the LG knockback flag / grapple mode from `ent->speed`; `Weapon_LightningFire` tests the player's flag; the per-frame refresh is removed. **Behaviour change:** (1) a change of `df_ob_KillOBs`, `df_hook_Type` or `df_lg_Knockback` takes effect with the next spawn of the player instead of the next frame (as in the original); (2) a lightning gun / grappling hook item with a `speed` key switches the LG knockback / grapple mode on for the player who picks it up (and a weapon without the key switches it off again for that player). Effect on the replay: sl1k-inh3-ok 74.07% -> 99.69% exact steps; lightning gun shots 0/37 -> 37/37 exact (incl. the LG knockback rocket). Revert: restore the per-frame block in `ClientThink_real` (clear the four bits, set them from `df_ob_killobs`, `df_hook_type & 1/2`, `df_lg_knockback`), delete the `G_SetDefragFlags` call in `ClientSpawn` and the two `speed` blocks in `Pickup_Weapon`, and test `df_lg_knockback.integer` in `Weapon_LightningFire`. Build clean (qagame, cgame, ui); repack the pk3.
- **Item pickup / gauntlet details found by the demo replay (g_items.c, g_active.c) -- FIXED** (testing/, `dfgolden gamesteps`; effect on the replay of the 20 CPM demos: the remaining item / event / weapon mismatches vanished, 98.9% -> 99.90% exact steps): (1) `Pickup_Powerup` (qagame `0x20afa`) starts the powerup timer at the exact `level.time` (`powerups[tag] = level.time`); the port used vanilla's `level.time - level.time % 1000`, so the networked powerup time differed. (2) `Touch_Item` (`0x20f11`) has no `predict = qfalse` for `IT_POWERUP`: the pickup event follows the client's `cg_predictItems` for every item type (the port had vanilla's powerup exception, which made a powerup pickup an external event instead of a predictable one). (3) `Add_Ammo` (`0x20c97`) clamps the ammo to at least -1 after the 200 cap (a negative item count takes ammo away, never below -1); the port could go to -99. (4) `ClientThink_real` (`0x1a9d9..0x1aa05`) calls `CheckGauntletAttack` (`0x288b1`) when the weapon is the gauntlet, the attack key is down, the talk key is up and `weaponTime <= 0`, and passes the result as `pm.gauntletHit` (the earlier docs said it was never called -- corrected in disasm/G_ACTIVE/Pmove_outer.md and BG_PMOVE/PM_Weapon.md); the port left `gauntletHit` at 0, so the gauntlet never fired. **Behaviour changes:** powerup timers (quad, haste, ...) are no longer aligned to whole seconds at pickup (the remaining time shown by cgame can differ by up to a second); powerup pickups are predicted like other items when `cg_predictItems` is 1; ammo never drops below -1; the gauntlet fires and hits again. Revert: (1) `level.time - ( level.time % 1000 )`, (2) re-add `predict = qfalse;` in the `IT_POWERUP` case, (3) remove the `< -1` clamp, (4) delete the gauntlet block after `memset (&pm, ...)` in `ClientThink_real`. Build clean (qagame, cgame, ui); repack the pk3.
- **VectorNormalize / VectorNormalize2 had the ioq3 arithmetic instead of Q3 1.32's (q_math.c; qagame, cgame prediction, ui) -- FIXED**: found by running the original DeFRaG `qagame.qvm` and the port side by side over the golden files (testing/, `dfgolden test --game <qvm>`): 76 steps (mostly 1-3 ulp differences in the origin, some velocity rounding flips on knockback steps) matched with the original and not with the port. The original `VectorNormalize` (qagame `0x9d12`) is `length = sqrt( x*x + y*y + z*z ); if ( length ) { ilength = 1 / length; v *= ilength; } return length;` (Q3 1.32); the port had ioq3's form `ilength = 1 / sqrt( length ); length *= ilength;`, which returns `x * ( 1 / sqrt( x ) )` instead of `sqrt( x )` (differs in the last bit for many inputs; the returned length is the wishspeed in PM_AirMove / PM_WalkMove / PM_FlyMove / PM_WaterMove, the speed in CPM_PM_Aircontrol and in the grapple / flight code). Same change in `VectorNormalize2`. **Behaviour change:** none that is visible without bit-exact comparison; the movement is now bit-exact with the original in the steps above (the golden files' 'port only' mismatches 78 -> 3). Revert: restore the two `ilength = 1/(float)sqrt (length); length *= ilength;` bodies. Build clean (qagame, cgame, ui); repack the pk3.
- **CG_BuildSolidList did not skip the items the viewed player has already taken (cg_predict.c) -- FIXED**: reported from a dedicated test server: powerups (haste on st1) gave "ghost" pickups over and over, with no effect. DeFRaG keeps every item in the world for everyone and marks it per client in `entityState_t.time` / `time2` (`G_ItemSetClientBit`, the server refuses a second pickup until the per-client cooldown is over). The original cgame stops the client from touching such an item in the prediction: `0x270e6` (CG_BuildSolidList) does `if ( cent->currentState.eType == ET_ITEM && !IsItemEntityAvailableToClient( cent, snap->ps.clientNum ) ) continue;` before the trigger / solid sorting (`0x4e14` = `CG_DF_ItemAvailable`, already ported for the drawing). The port's CG_BuildSolidList was the vanilla one, so every frame spent on a taken item predicted EV_ITEM_PICKUP again (sound / effect each time, the server gives nothing); powerups showed it most because `BG_CanItemBeGrabbed` is always true for them (health / armor are refused at full health). Same fix is in `CG_DF_ItemAvailable` made non-static (cg_ents.c, declared in cg_local.h). **Behaviour change:** taken items no longer fire client-side pickup prediction (what the original does). Revert: remove the `ET_ITEM && !CG_DF_ItemAvailable` `continue` in CG_BuildSolidList. Build clean (qagame, cgame, ui); repack the pk3 (cgame changed; the dedicated server only needs it for clients, but the client downloads / uses this cgame).
- **Delayed-restart countdown ("Free For All / Starts in: 3 2 1") is not drawn on DeFRaG servers (cg_draw.c) -- CHANGED to match the original's observed behaviour; mechanism OPEN (Q8)**: a vote that passes runs `map_restart 3`; the engine publishes the restart time as `CS_WARMUP` (g_doWarmup 0), and the oDFs client drew the vanilla countdown with its 3-2-1 voices right after `cv`. The user checked the original DeFRaG: the vote passes at once (one player) but only the "prepare to fight" voice plays, no text and no counting voices. The decode shows no reason (the original `CG_DrawWarmup` 0x1cb7e / `CG_ParseWarmup` 0x2829a / `CG_Draw2D` 0x1cd86 are vanilla and the original qagame sets CS_WARMUP only in CheckTournament and the worldspawn setup, as oDFs does), so this is a behaviour match, not a decode: `CG_Draw2D` skips `CG_DrawWarmup` while `cg.warmup` is set and `CG_DF_IsDefragServer()` (now non-static, cg_local.h); `CG_ParseWarmup` is unchanged, so the "prepare" voice still plays. Non-DeFRaG servers keep the vanilla countdown. See disasm/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md Q8. Revert: remove the `cg.warmup && CG_DF_IsDefragServer()` condition in CG_Draw2D. Build clean; repack the pk3.
- **CG_MapRestart played the "fight" sound and printed "FIGHT!" after every restart (cg_servercmds.c) -- FIXED**: a passed vote ends in a (delayed) `map_restart`; after it the oDFs client printed the big "FIGHT!" and played fight.wav (the ioq3 form of vanilla's tournament-only code, with the gametype test commented out). The original cgame has no "FIGHT!" string at all, and `sound/feedback/fight.wav` is registered (`0x3c5`, handle `0x114338`) but the handle is never read anywhere, so DeFRaG shows and plays nothing there. Removed the block. **Behaviour change:** no "FIGHT!" text / voice after map_restart (also on non-DeFRaG servers: it only ever applied to the tournament in vanilla). Revert: put the five lines back. Found while chasing the missing-countdown report (entry 88). Build clean; repack the pk3.
- **CHS item 227 "overbounces" always showed "off" (cg_dfchs.c) -- FIXED**: the item read `defrag_obs` from the SERVERINFO configstring, but the qagame publishes it as a SYSTEMINFO cvar (`CVAR_SYSTEMINFO | LATCH | ROM`, set in G_ResolveDfMode: 1 = overbounces on), and the original caches it from the systeminfo (`0x11d2b4`, see `CG_DF_SvFacts`). The key was never found, so the stat said "off" whatever `df_ob_KillOBs` was (also after a `cv kill_OBs 0`). Now reads `sys`. The server side (vote -> `df_ob_killobs N; map_restart 3`, `pm.obs_disabled`, DFLAG_OBS_DISABLED at spawn) was already correct. Revert: read `srv` again. Build clean; repack the pk3.
